# Existing reduce helpers — migration and test coverage

Branch: `malimpic/migrate-to-host-reduce-helpers` · Commit: `f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73` · Audit date: 2026-09-08.

**Compute scope: 125 existing explicit `compute_kernel_lib::reduce<...>(...)` helper calls in 58 source files.** Calls already using `reduce<Call>()`, raw compute APIs, specialized reductions and direct LLK calls are excluded from the compute inventory.

**Dataflow extension: 100 old scaler/partial-helper calls in 68 source files, reached by 69 dataflow kernel bodies.** The separate dataflow section also counts manual auxiliary producers and additional factories/test builders, giving 218 migration candidates, or 220 including retirement of the explicit compute API. Raw compute calls remain outside the inventory even when their dataflow partners use an old helper.

**The compute-side test audit covers 60 factory implementations in 59 host files.** One file (F087) contains two factories and has two rows. Two further host files are retained as relationships: F084 is a shared builder and F024 is a non-executing source-composition test. Three compute-helper source files have no static host reference.

## Coverage summary

The report distinguishes **tests of the specific factory** from **tests that execute its retained helper path**. “Neither” means neither sanity nor L2 nightly; the row distinguishes tests in other pipelines, disabled tests, and unreachable helper branches. A test of another factory using the same kernel is not credited.

| CI classification | Factory has an enabled test | Test executes retained helper path |
|---|---|---|
| Both | 15 | 14 |
| Sanity only | 6 | 5 |
| L2 nightly only | 32 | 33 |
| Neither | 7 | 8 |

**This is a static routing and CI-configuration audit, not a test run or a coverage-instrumentation result.** Test parameters, factory/kernel dispatch, skips, pytest filters and available CI SKUs were checked at the pinned commit. “Yes” establishes at least one relevant executable case; it does not claim every template specialization or branch is covered. Broad model tests are not treated as proof of a specific factory without a traced operation test.

Key findings:

- F034 (distributed DiT RMSNorm) and F036 (attention-residual gather-softmax) have numerical tests in other CI pipelines; neither is scheduled by the audited sanity/L2 commands.
- F040’s indexer helper fallback is explicitly tested in L2. Sanity’s indexer selections do not reach it.
- F041 (ring indexer) and F086 (Welford post-all-gather) reference helper-bearing sources, but their retained helper branches cannot be enabled by those factories.
- F046–F048 (Quasar) have no direct factory tests found. F090 (general large-height softmax) has a disabled smoke test, not enabled coverage.
- The five Python example/toy factories run in sanity’s helper-library group. That group has no P100 SKU and is not included by L2’s P100 sanity rerun.
- Standard interleaved distributed-normalization cases and the 2D pre-all-gather factory have single-device simulation tests in L2. Their similarly named legacy or eight-device unit tests do not establish coverage in single-card sanity.

## Dataflow helpers and total migration file scope

**The previous numbers were 58 compute-kernel source files and 59 factory files.** The 58 compute sources include three Python files with inline C++; each of those files also contains a dataflow body and its factory. Kernel bodies and physical files therefore have different totals.

**69 dataflow kernel bodies use the old reduction helpers: 67 directly and two through a shared header. There are 100 unique old dataflow-helper call sites in 68 physical files.** The direct-call files are 64 C++ kernel files, three Python inline-source files and one shared header. The two DiT writer entry files add indirect users; including that header and both entries gives 70 dataflow-related files.

The dataflow helpers prepare scaler/partial tiles for compute; these are not compute `reduce(...)` calls. This repository-wide scan also finds dataflow consumers paired with raw/SFPU/Welford compute code, so it extends beyond the earlier 58-source compute-helper inventory. Existing factory CI findings remain attached to their original factories; new DH entries below have not been assigned CI coverage by this count-only extension.

| Old dataflow API | Unique call sites |
|---|---|
| <code>calculate&#95;and&#95;prepare&#95;reduce&#95;scaler</code> | 75 |
| <code>prepare&#95;partial&#95;reduce&#95;scalers</code> | 1 |
| <code>prepare&#95;reduce&#95;scaler</code> | 24 |

The new dataflow API is [prepare_reduce_auxiliary_tiles<Auxiliary>()](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/ttnn/cpp/ttnn/kernel_lib/reduce_helpers_dataflow.hpp#L22>). Already-migrated uses of that API are excluded. `prepare_zero_tile` is still provided by [l1_helpers.hpp](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/ttnn/cpp/ttnn/kernel_lib/l1_helpers.hpp#L62>) and is not an old reduce-helper consumer. Likewise, similarly named standalone/model scaler utilities are outside these three APIs.

### Revised file estimate

**218 distinct consumer, auxiliary-producer, factory and test-builder files are migration candidates.** Budget **220** if retiring the explicit compute overload is also included: its declaration and implementation/forwarding live in two additional library files. This is a source-level planning count, not a guarantee that every candidate will need an edit; shared wrappers, inactive branches and unreferenced kernels can change the eventual diff.

| Scope increment | New unique files | Running total | Accounting |
|---|---|---|---|
| Original compute/host inventory | 115 | 115 | 58 compute sources + 59 factory files + one shared builder − three overlapping inline Python files. |
| Additional direct dataflow consumer files | 65 | 180 | 68 call-bearing files − three Python files already counted. Includes the shared DiT header and three test kernels. |
| Dataflow entries using that shared header | 2 | 182 | Both DiT writer bodies participate in the auxiliary-descriptor integration. |
| Additional manual auxiliary producers | 17 | 199 | Existing readers fill reduction scalers themselves; none calls the old dataflow helpers. |
| Additional host/test builder files | 19 | 218 | Factories of the extra dataflow users, including two C++ test builders. |
| Conditional: retire explicit compute API | 2 | 220 | reduce_helpers_compute.hpp and reduce_helpers_compute.inl. |

**Tests:** five directly affected test files are already inside the 218: three UDM dataflow kernels and their two C++ program builders. The existing operation tests are validation targets, not automatically changed files. F024’s source-composition test is excluded because it does not execute the helper body. New regression tests or CI edits have no determined file count until implementation establishes a need.

**Shared library:** the planned host, compute and dataflow interfaces already exist. This call-site audit has not established additional required host/dataflow-library edits. Retiring the explicit compute entry is separate: its [declaration](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/ttnn/cpp/ttnn/kernel_lib/reduce_helpers_compute.hpp#L646>) and [implementation](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/ttnn/cpp/ttnn/kernel_lib/reduce_helpers_compute.inl#L979>) must be considered together because [reduce<Call>() currently forwards into that backend](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/ttnn/cpp/ttnn/kernel_lib/reduce_helpers_compute.inl#L1605>). Do not delete the backend as if it were an unused consumer.

### Readers that construct reduction auxiliaries manually

These 17 readers are additional integration candidates for the original compute-helper migration. They produce the scaler/ones tile consumed by a retained compute helper, but do not call any of the three old dataflow APIs. Replacing compute with a host-planned call requires its matching auxiliary recipe; preserve unrelated uses of shared constants.

The dataflow scan used the same tracked first-party C/C++/header/inline-Python scope as the compute audit, stripped comments and declarations, resolved shared-header inclusion, and checked host path references. Counts are pinned to <code>f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73</code>. Reports and temporary audit scripts are excluded from the migration file count. No migration code, device tests or builds were run for this reporting step.

## Kernel call counts and accumulation patterns

**58 kernel sources, 125 static helper call sites.** Seven sources carry a running reduction through a loop; eight combine a fixed prefix and tail; nineteen perform multiple statistics/stages; twenty-four use one helper invocation per independent local reduction. The table below separates the mechanisms used to combine results.

A **static site** is one explicit helper expression in the inventory. A **runtime call** is an invocation of that helper, including calls reached through wrappers. Counts below are per compute core’s stated work unit (row, output tile, shard or invocation), not necessarily per whole distributed tensor. They exclude raw reductions and tile traversal inside the helper. Small shapes can collapse an accumulation loop to one iteration.

Here, **cross-call accumulation** means carrying a running reduced result into the next call for the same reduction, either through `Accumulate` or a callback. A later fresh bulk reduction over a collection of partials is classified as a separate stage. An outer loop over unrelated rows, or a manual tile-add loop before one final helper call, does not imply helper-result carry.

The accumulator contract says iteration zero does not reload a prior result; later iterations do. See [Accumulate documentation](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/ttnn/cpp/ttnn/kernel_lib/reduce_helpers_compute.hpp#L375>). Every source is assigned exactly one category, prioritizing loop carry, then fixed prefix/tail combination, then multiple stages, then a single independent reduction. Runtime alternatives and additional stages are described in its row.

[Download all 58 kernel classifications](kernel_reduce_patterns.csv). Source IDs link to the exact retained helper expressions; factory IDs link to the existing test and CI audit. Classification describes the source’s helper paths, including sources with no execution coverage; it does not establish that all configurations execute them.

| Pattern | Kernel sources | Static helper sites |
|---|---|---|
| [Loop accumulation using Accumulate](#pattern-loop-api) | 6 | 17 |
| [Loop accumulation inside a callback](#pattern-loop-callback) | 1 | 1 |
| [Fixed prefix and tail using Accumulate](#pattern-pair-api) | 6 | 26 |
| [Fixed prefix and tail combined manually](#pattern-pair-manual) | 2 | 8 |
| [Multiple statistics or reduction stages](#pattern-stages) | 19 | 42 |
| [One helper call per independent reduction](#pattern-single) | 24 | 31 |
| **Total** | **58** | **125** |

### Static call-site distribution

| Sites in a source | Kernel sources | Total sites |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 24 | 24 |
| 2 | 16 | 32 |
| 3 | 7 | 21 |
| 4 | 7 | 28 |
| 5 | 4 | 20 |

The static count alone misses both directions: [S094](#s094) has one site that carries MAX/SUM through chunk loops; [S104](#s104) has one site invoked three times for different reductions. Conversely, [S109](#s109) has three dimension alternatives but runs only one helper per iteration. The five sites in each Moreh softmax kernel execute as two or three calls per work item.

**Accumulate API usage: 12 sources.** Six use it in a loop and six only for a fixed tail. [S094](#s094) implements loop carry through its callback despite passing `NoAccumulation`; [S074](#s074) and [S076](#s076) manually add two helper outputs on their multi-tile LOG paths.

<a id="pattern-loop-api"></a>

### Loop accumulation using Accumulate

The same reduction continues across chunks or tiles. Iteration zero initializes a fresh result; later iterations reload the accumulator.

| Kernel / static sites | Runtime helper calls and work unit | Accumulator, stages and alternatives | Control-flow evidence / host references |
|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="pattern-s047"></a>**[S047](#s047)** [Quasar generic row-major reduction](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/reduction/generic/device/kernels/compute/reduce_rm.cpp#L47>)<br>2 static sites: [R162](#r162), [R163](#r163) | W: ceil(Wt / wt_tiles_per_chunk) calls per H chunk. H: ceil(Ht / ht_tiles_per_chunk) calls per local output tile. | Accumulate::at(cb_acc, chunk_idx). One of the two sites runs per chunk: intermediate results go to the accumulator; the last goes to the output, with optional post-multiply. chunk_idx resets to zero for each H chunk (W reduction) or output tile (H reduction). A one-chunk case makes one call. | [final/intermediate alternatives](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/reduction/generic/device/kernels/compute/reduce_rm.cpp#L47>); [W loop and reset](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/reduction/generic/device/kernels/compute/reduce_rm.cpp#L104>); [H loop and reset](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/reduction/generic/device/kernels/compute/reduce_rm.cpp#L128>)<br>Host references: [F047](#f047) |

<a id="pattern-loop-callback"></a>

### Loop accumulation inside a callback

The helper receives NoAccumulation, but its callback explicitly merges the preceding result. This still carries reduction state across calls.

| Kernel / static sites | Runtime helper calls and work unit | Accumulator, stages and alternatives | Control-flow evidence / host references |
|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="pattern-s094"></a>**[S094](#s094)** [Attention softmax — large tensor](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/softmax/device/kernels/attention/compute/softmax_large_tensor.cpp#L260>)<br>1 static site: [R296](#r296) | P calls for SUM, plus P for MAX when NUMERIC_STABLE; P = num_dfb_passes, per row (NCHt rows). | One templated site is reused for both statistics and ping-pong buffers. The post-reduce callback waits on the previous output, loads it into DST[1], combines by MAX or ADD, then pops it. use_prev_reduce=false starts each row/statistic; later passes set it true. NoAccumulation does not make these calls independent. | [callback carry](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/softmax/device/kernels/attention/compute/softmax_large_tensor.cpp#L258>); [ping-pong dispatch](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/softmax/device/kernels/attention/compute/softmax_large_tensor.cpp#L291>); [row/reset and MAX loop](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/softmax/device/kernels/attention/compute/softmax_large_tensor.cpp#L392>); [SUM reset and loop](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/softmax/device/kernels/attention/compute/softmax_large_tensor.cpp#L439>)<br>Host references: [F088](#f088) |

<a id="pattern-single"></a>

### One helper call per independent reduction

Each local reduction is finished by one helper invocation. Outer loops process independent rows, columns, slices or benchmark repetitions. Any tile accumulation before that invocation is described separately.

| Kernel / static sites | Runtime helper calls and work unit | Accumulator, stages and alternatives | Control-flow evidence / host references |

## Factory test coverage

Sanity and L2 columns classify the listed **factory tests**. The assessment explicitly flags F040’s sanity tests and F041’s ring tests, which do not execute the retained helper. The CSV also includes separate factory-test and helper-path classifications. CI group links include exact commands and hardware filters.

## Complete retained compute-helper call list

Every retained compute-helper call is listed below with its source expression (whitespace collapsed). The same source may be used by several factories; consult each factory row for selection and CI coverage. Runtime loops are one syntactic call site, not one row per iteration. Python inline C++ expressions use the enclosing Python file’s line numbers.

## CI definitions and commands

Sanity is traced from [sanity-tests.yaml](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/.github/workflows/sanity-tests.yaml#L418>) through TTNN and multi-card sanity implementations. L2 is the scheduled [tt-metal-l2-nightly.yaml](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/.github/workflows/tt-metal-l2-nightly.yaml#L212>) graph, including its [P100 sanity rerun](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/.github/workflows/tt-metal-l2-nightly.yaml#L276>). Other model/e2e workflows are reported separately. Commented commands and disabled/skipped cases are not counted.

The reusable TTNN sanity workflow reads [ttnn_sanity_tests.yaml](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/.github/workflows/ttnn-sanity-tests-impl.yaml#L83>); the ops workflow reads [ops_unit_tests.yaml](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/.github/workflows/ops-unit-tests-impl.yaml#L27>). The scheduled L2 category selection includes fused, reduction, experimental, moreh, transformers, misc and data_movement. Individual manual workflow runs can choose smaller subsets.

<a id="s_fused"></a>

### S_FUSED — ttnn fused group 1

Lane: **Sanity**. Definition: [tests/pipeline_reorg/ttnn_sanity_tests.yaml](<https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-metal/blob/f808380a87b320e24457c600cc79b05d7a0b8f73/tests/pipeline_reorg/ttnn_sanity_tests.yaml#L170>). SKUs: <code>wh&#95;n300&#95;civ2</code>, <code>bh&#95;p100</code>, <code>bh&#95;p150b&#95;civ2</code>, <code>sim&#95;wh&#95;n150</code>, <code>sim&#95;bh&#95;p150</code>.

```bash
pytest --timeout 300 tests/ttnn/unit_tests/operations/fused -xv --splits 2 --group 1 --splitting-algorithm least_duration -m "not disable_fast_runtime_mode"
```

Group 2 at line 186 runs the other half; together both splits cover the directory. Physical SKUs include wh_n300_civ2, bh_p100 and bh_p150b_civ2. Eight-device tests cannot run on these SKUs.

## Audit method and limits

The original repository-wide syntax inventory was filtered to category `explicit_helper`. Host mappings were filtered by retained sources, and the reduce-accumulate descriptor for its manual SFPU string was removed. Shared source files remain listed once, with all statically identified host references. No reduction implementation was changed.

The kernel-pattern section traces all 58 sources through enclosing loops, wrapper invocations, template/preprocessor alternatives, accumulator aliases, post-reduce callbacks and manual combination steps. Runtime counts are conditional source-level counts at the explicit helper boundary, not measured executions or a proposed count of descriptors for the new interface. Each source appears in exactly one pattern category and all 125 retained sites are accounted for.

For each factory, test calls and parameter sets were compared with the device-operation selector and the kernel-source/compile-argument choices. The audit checked pytest `-k` / `-m` filters, ignored files, module/function skips, disabled C++ tests, simulated versus physical device counts, and pipeline SKU lists. It records representative direct numerical/cache tests rather than an exhaustive list of every indirect model invocation. “No test found” is a repository-search result, not proof that external tests do not exist.

No device tests or builds were run for this report-only change. The report artifacts were checked for retained-call completeness, factory-audit completeness, valid local evidence paths/lines, CSV consistency, and HTML links/anchors. CI membership describes configuration at the pinned commit, not a successful CI run.

## Runnable migration unit-test suite

**984 test definitions: 899 Python functions and 85 C++ cases.** Pytest collected **18,452 parametrized cases** across 169 source groups; 0 groups failed collection. Four C++ cases are disabled upstream and remain skipped by default. This expands the representative factory/CI audit into a runnable operation-unit suite, including the dataflow-extension factories.

Run from the repository root with `python scripts/run_reduce_migration_tests.py`. The script invokes `scripts/run_safe_pytest.sh`, keeps per-group results, continues after failures, and supports `--collect-only`, `--list`, `--factory`, and `--group`. Device unit-test bodies have not been run for this inventory.

[HTML suite report](unit_test_suite.html) · [Suite documentation](unit_test_suite.md) · [Runner manifest](unit_test_suite.json) · [Every selected test](unit_test_suite.csv) · [Collected counts](unit_test_collection.json)

## Reduce-helper kernel sanity subset

The [sanity report](sanity_test_suite.html) selects **74 exact cases** (58 Python + 16 C++) covering **129 kernel entries**: 51 compute, 61 dataflow and all 17 manual auxiliary readers. Fifteen entries have no executable test path and remain explicit gaps. Each covered kernel has one primary case; cases shared by compute/readers/writers are deduplicated.

Run `python scripts/run_reduce_migration_sanity.py --list` to inspect cases and hardware lanes. Use `--lane common --lane wormhole` or `--lane common --lane blackhole` for single-device selections; other lanes need their documented hardware. All cases use `run_safe_pytest`; skipped execution does not count as passing sanity. See the [manifest](sanity_test_suite.json) and [kernel coverage CSV](sanity_kernel_coverage.csv).
